91:"$Sreact.suspense"
4:T99d,When a multi-step orchestrator action (e.g. TASKS provision_workspace → spawn_agent → submit_workspace) returns a partial-success ActionResult, the post-action evaluator keeps emitting `{ success: false, decision: "CONTINUE" }` and the planner re-dispatches the same first step instead of moving to the next. The loop runs until the repeated-failure or trajectory limit fires; on Discord the user sees the 120s timeout fallback.

## Repro

1. Configure 2PM-style orchestrator agent with the TASKS surface (plugin-agent-orchestrator) and a valid GITHUB_TOKEN.
2. DM the agent: `build me hello-world from scratch under github <org>` against a freshly-created empty repo.
3. Tail logs:

```
ACTION_STARTED actionName=TASKS                                # ← provision_workspace succeeds
decision=CONTINUE, messageToUser="On it. Provisioning..."     # ← evaluator does not see provision as terminal
PLAN_ACTIONS action=TASKS subaction=provision_workspace        # ← LLM picks provision_workspace AGAIN
ACTION_STARTED actionName=TASKS                                # ← second provision
decision=CONTINUE
...
TrajectoryLimitExceeded: Repeated tool failure limit exceeded for TASKS
```

8 successive `ACTION_STARTED actionName=TASKS` events for the same turn, each with `op=provision_workspace`. spawn_agent is never reached.

## Likely cause

- The post-action evaluator reads `success` from the action result. The orchestrator op handlers (`runProvisionWorkspace`) return `{ success: true, ... }` on the workspace path, but the planner-loop appears to discard the prior tool result by the time the next planner turn renders the available_actions block — so the LLM does not see "you already provisioned a workspace, now spawn the agent."
- Either the per-turn action-results provider is not engaged, or the evaluator routes CONTINUE without surfacing the prior `success: true` result back into the next planner prompt.

## Asks

1. Confirm whether `decision: CONTINUE` is supposed to short-circuit re-planning around the just-executed action or trigger an entirely fresh PLAN_ACTIONS pass.
2. If fresh: where should successful intermediate action results be surfaced to the next planner turn so the LLM knows to advance to the next op rather than restart from the first?

Happy to file a PR once a maintainer confirms the intended semantics; not obvious from reading planner-loop.ts alone.

🤖 Filed via [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)5:Tccb,## Summary

For some prompts the bot posts **multiple separate user-facing messages** for a single user request because the orchestrator spawns retry sub-agents (on \`state_lost\`, on dead-URL verification, on error) and **every** sub-agent's \`task_complete\` event posts to Discord. The user sees 2-3+ replies with overlapping or junky content (random page sub-resources, duplicate URLs, etc.).

## Repro on 2026-05-25 07:05-07:06 UTC

User prompt: \`@bot what is the current stable python version\` (msg \`1508365173918793882\`)

Parent trajectory \`tj-f34087ff6800b9\`: iters=1 tools=1 — **ONE** \`TASKS:spawn_agent\` call.

**Sub-agent trajectories fired**:
- \`tj-f3e500c94869d9\` — **blocked** (external_directory)
- \`tj-f3e1bdae0bf33b\` — **error** ("Sub-agent state was lost (process exited without persisting); spawn a fresh sub-agent to continue.")
- \`tj-f4348d0432b057\` — **task_complete** (python.org/downloads + psf + pypi + donate + linkedin URLs)
- \`tj-f4823d19a147b7\` — **task_complete** (analytics.python.org/js/script.file-downloads.outbound-links.js)
- \`tj-f4b3d723dc9ee1\` — **task_complete** (python.org/downloads + python-3.14.5 tarball)

**User-visible Discord messages** (all replying to the same prompt):
1. 07:05:46: "I don't have live access to retrieve the latest stable Python version number right now. https://www.python.org/downloads/"
2. 07:06:07: "https://analytics.python.org/js/script.file-downloads.outbound-links.js"
3. 07:06:19: "https://www.python.org/downloads/"

The second message is **page sub-resource leakage** — analytics.python.org tracker URL that has no business reaching the user.

## Why suppression doesn't catch it

\`sub-agent-router.ts:566\` suppresses post when \`event === "task_complete" && deadUrls.length > 0\` and a retry is dispatched. When all URLs verify OK (python.org returns 200, analytics also returns 200 with content-type=js), \`deadUrls.length === 0\`. The retry-suppression branch is skipped, and every task_complete posts. The session-tracking set \`verifyRetryHandedOffSessions\` only catches the verify-retry path, not the state-lost-retry or other-retry paths.

## Suggested fix

Structural:
1. Track \`firstTaskCompletePostedForOriginMessage\` — when the first task_complete for a parent prompt posts to user, mark it. Subsequent task_completes for the same parent prompt (i.e. retries that completed after the first one already posted) should be silently absorbed.
2. Filter sub-agent task report text to drop URLs to known tracker/analytics hosts (analytics.*, ga.jspm.io, beacon.*, etc.) — these are never user-relevant.

Alternative (less invasive): in \`retryIncompleteBuild\`, also suppress when the retry is for state-lost or for any reason other than verification failure.

## Acceptance

- One user prompt → at most one user-facing reply, regardless of how many internal retries the orchestrator does
- Sub-agent task reports never leak analytics/tracker URLs to Discord
- Existing verify-retry behavior preserved for legitimate dev-app workflows (where the first attempt produced dead URLs and the retry produced working URLs)

Filed via live deepscan on milady deployment 2026-05-25 (user nubs caught the 3-message Discord output that the probe script missed).6:T638,## Summary

On "what is the latest version of Node.js" the bot replied with model-internals language:

> "I don't have real-time access to check the current Node.js release, but as of my last update (June 2024) the latest stable major version was Node 22..."

The phrase **"as of my last update (June 2024)"** is the underlying LLM's training-cutoff bleeding through. The bot is supposed to be Remilio Nubilio, a Discord agent — not a model with a training cutoff. Breaks character + dates wrong (today is 2026-05-25).

## Trajectory

Live probe 2026-05-25, \`tj-cc6a6b7f2609ce.json\`. Stage 0: \`simple=true, requiresTool=false\`. Bot replied directly without using BROWSER to fetch nodejs.org.

## Suggested approach

Two routes:
1. **Stage 1 routing**: for "what is the latest/current X" with a temporal hint, set \`requiresTool=true\` with \`candidateActions=["BROWSER"]\` so the planner actually fetches.
2. **Prompt rule in messageHandlerTemplate**: ban model-internals phrasing in replyText ("my last update", "my training data", "as a language model", "I was trained on", "knowledge cutoff", etc.) — replace with "I don't have live access — try checking X" or actually use BROWSER.

(1) is structurally cleaner; (2) is a backup for cases where (1) doesn't route.

## Acceptance

- "what is the latest version of nodejs" either fetches via BROWSER or honestly says "I don't track that live; check nodejs.org/en/about/releases".
- No replies containing "my last update", "trained on", or other model-internals phrasing.

8:Tf28,## Summary

After working around #8000 by installing `@elizaos/cli` directly (`bun add -g @elizaos/cli`), the CLI still crashes at boot because `engine.io` (transitive) calls `(0, debug_1.default)("engine:transport")` and `debug_1.default` is `undefined` under Bun's CJS resolver. The boot path eagerly loads `engine.io` even for commands that do not need a server (`create`, `--version`, etc).

## Repro

```bash
bun add -g @elizaos/cli      # installs @elizaos/cli@1.7.2 (npm latest)
elizaos create my-app --template project
```

```
3 | exports.Transport = void 0;
4 | const events_1 = require("events");
5 | const parser_v4 = require("engine.io-parser");
6 | const parser_v3 = require("./parser-v3/index");
7 | const debug_1 = require("debug");
8 | const debug = (0, debug_1.default)("engine:transport");
                       ^
TypeError: (0, debug_1.default) is not a function. (In '(0, debug_1.default)("engine:transport")',
'(0, debug_1.default)' is undefined)
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/transport.js:8:27
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/transports/polling.js:3:1
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/transports/index.js:2:1
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/server.js:431:5
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/engine.io.js:6:1

Bun v1.3.10 (macOS arm64)
```

## Root cause

`engine.io`'s compiled `transport.js` emits:

```js
const debug_1 = require("debug");
const debug = (0, debug_1.default)("engine:transport");
```

It assumes a `.default` property on the `debug` CJS module. `debug@4.x` exports the factory directly (`module.exports = createDebug`); there is no `.default` property in the CJS shape. Node's CJS-from-ESM interop synthesizes a `default` property automatically; Bun's resolver does not produce the same shape for this dual-package module under the global-bin code path. This is a known engine.io + Bun interop class, not an elizaOS source-level bug.

## Why this is elizaOS's problem in practice

`@elizaos/cli@1.7.2` eagerly loads `engine.io` / Socket.IO at module-init time of *any* command — even `--help` and `create` walk through `engine.io/build/engine.io.js` → `server.js` → `transports/index.js` → `transport.js` before the command's own logic runs. Commands that don't need a websocket server should not be loading the websocket server stack at parse time.

## Environment

- @elizaos/cli@1.7.2 (`npm view @elizaos/cli dist-tags.latest`)
- Node v24.13.0
- Bun v1.3.10
- macOS arm64

## Suggested mitigations (any one would unblock)

1. **Lazy-load `engine.io` / Socket.IO off the boot path.** Move the server imports behind the commands that actually serve (`start`, `dev`). `create`, `info`, `upgrade`, `--version`, `--help` should not transitively touch `engine.io`. This is also a startup-latency win independent of the Bun bug.
2. **Pin / patch the transitive dep.** Either pin `engine.io` to a build whose compiled output uses `__importDefault`, or pin `debug` to a version that exports a named `default` property.
3. **Document Bun support explicitly.** If Bun is a supported runtime for the global CLI, gate publish on a `bun`-runtime smoke (see #8000). If not, the install instructions should say so.

## Status on develop

Verified: develop's `packages/elizaos@2.0.3` scaffolds a project end-to-end via Node (`node packages/elizaos/dist/cli.js create … --template project --yes` succeeds against a fresh `bun install` of the workspace). Either the boot was trimmed or the dep tree was restructured — would be useful for maintainers to surface what changed, since that change is the basis for closing this on the next stable release.

## Workaround

Same as #8000 — use the develop CLI directly until a release supersedes 1.7.2 on `latest`.9:Tc15,## Summary

The current `npm dist-tag latest` for both `elizaos` and `@elizaos/cli` is `1.7.2`. A clean global install fails on the very first command because the `elizaos` alias package's bin shim imports a subpath that `@elizaos/cli@1.7.2`'s `exports` map does not whitelist.

## Repro

```bash
bun add -g elizaos        # or: npm i -g elizaos
elizaos --version         # any subcommand reproduces
```

```
Error [ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED]: Package subpath './dist/index.js' is not defined by "exports" in
/Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/@elizaos/cli/package.json
imported from /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/elizaos/bin/elizaos.js
    at exportsNotFound (node:internal/modules/esm/resolve:313:10)
    at packageExportsResolve (node:internal/modules/esm/resolve:661:9)
    ...
  code: 'ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED'
```

## Root cause (verified from npm registry)

`elizaos@1.7.2` ships `bin/elizaos.js`:

```js
import '@elizaos/cli/dist/index.js';
```

`@elizaos/cli@1.7.2` declares:

```json
"exports": {
  ".": "./dist/index.js",
  "./package.json": "./package.json"
}
```

Node's ESM resolver enforces `exports` strictly — `./dist/index.js` as a subpath is blocked even though the file exists. The shim should import the package root (`'@elizaos/cli'`), not a subpath.

## Environment

- elizaos@1.7.2 (`latest` tag)
- @elizaos/cli@1.7.2 (`latest` tag)
- Node v24.13.0
- Bun v1.3.10
- macOS arm64

Confirmed from npm registry:

```
$ npm view elizaos dist-tags
{ latest: '1.7.2', next: '2.0.0-alpha.32', alpha: '2.0.0-alpha.535', beta: '2.0.0-beta.5' }

$ npm view @elizaos/cli dist-tags
{ beta: '1.7.2-beta.0', latest: '1.7.2', alpha: '1.7.3-alpha.4' }
```

## Status on develop

Develop has already restructured: `packages/elizaos` is the CLI itself at v2.0.3; no alias shim, no inter-package subpath import. `@elizaos/cli@1.7.3-alpha.4` also no longer has a separate shim — its `bin` points directly to `dist/index.js`. The structural fix is in; the gap is that no release has superseded the broken `latest` tag.

## What would prevent regression

A pre-publish global-install smoke test:

1. `npm pack` → tarball
2. `npm i -g <tarball>` into a sandboxed prefix
3. Run `<prefix>/bin/elizaos --version` under **both** Node and Bun
4. Assert exit code 0

The existing `packages/elizaos/scripts/packaged-smoke.mjs` does a local install but not a global one — global install resolves bin via a different path that is stricter on `exports`. Happy to send a PR.

Optional bonus: a static linter that walks `packages/*/package.json`, finds any `bin`-listed file doing `import 'foo/sub/path.js'`, and verifies `foo`'s `exports` map permits that subpath. Would catch this entire class of bug pre-publish.

```bash

bun install
bun run --cwd packages/elizaos build
node packages/elizaos/dist/cli.js create my-app --template project --yes

# Or pin a pre-release tag:
npm i -g elizaos@beta

b:T257d,## Summary

Adds `@elizaos/plugin-gitpathologist` — a per-surface forensic git-history analyzer for elizaOS agents.

- **What it does:** scans `git log` for a path/glob surface, classifies each commit (conventional-commit rules), scores per-commit health deltas + a running EMA, finds peaks (local health maxima) and drift inflections (commits after which the score sustains a downturn), and writes a post-mortem `RotCause` for each drift.
- **Pipeline shape:** deterministic spine (`scan` → `classify` → `score` → `inflect`), LLM only for `narrate`, with a deterministic fallback when no `runtime.useModel` is available. All commit text + diff snippets pass through a local secret scrubber (`sk-ant-`, `sk-`, `ghp_`, `AKIA`, `Bearer`, env-name patterns) before leaving the process.
- **API:** single multiplex `GIT_PATHOLOGY` action with operations `report` (default), `list`, `trace` (v2 stub), `diff` (v2 stub). Reports cached to `<repo>/.milady/gitpathology/<sha256(surface+since)>.json`, freshness keyed on HEAD sha.
- **Wiring:** added to `OPTIONAL_CORE_PLUGINS`, gated by `gitpathologistRequested()` alongside the orchestrator gate. Resolution order: config agent entry → defaults → `ELIZA_GITPATHOLOGIST` env var → auto-on when `<cwd>/.git` exists. Mobile platforms never load it. `gitpathologist?: boolean` added to `AgentEntryConfig`, `AgentDefaultsConfig`, and both zod schemas.

## Why

Forensic "when did this surface look best, when did it veer off, and why" analysis is something every long-lived monorepo needs and nobody has a clean plugin for. `plugin-github` covers PR/issue ops, not commit-history pathology. This fills that gap with cheap deterministic detection and optional LLM narration — pairs naturally with spatial codebase tools.

## Test plan

- [x] **Unit + e2e:** 45/45 vitest pass in `plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/`. Coverage: `secret-scrubber`, `classify` (conventional + WIP + revert + merge + risk flags), `score` (deltas, EMA, revert-proximity penalty), `inflect` (peaks, drifts, threshold filtering), `cache` (key stability, round-trip, freshness), `scan` (real git output via toy-repo fixture), full pipeline e2e on a synthetic A(clean)→B(WIP dump)→C(churn-spiral)→D(recovery) repo.
- [x] **Typecheck:** `tsc --noEmit` clean for the plugin (same 5 pre-existing `@elizaos/core` codegen errors as plugin-mcp's baseline — not introduced by this PR).
- [x] **Dogfood:** Ran [`scripts/dogfood.ts`](https://github.com/2-A-M/eliza/blob/milady/plugin-gitpathologist/plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/scripts/dogfood.ts) against `packages/agent/src/runtime` over a 60-day window. 119 commits scanned in ~160ms, 5 plausible peaks and 3 plausible drift inflections detected, including a 31,403-line "chore: restore current develop tree" correctly flagged as the largest drift.
- [ ] **Future:** v2 `trace` (symptom → introducing-commit walk) and `diff` (compare two surfaces / windows) operations; LLM-batch classify pass to refine "other" → real category on non-conventional commit subjects.

## Out of scope for v1 (deliberate)

- Cross-surface dependency graph (Understand-Anything territory)
- PR-level aggregation (commit-level is the load-bearing primitive)
- Author-level metrics (privacy/political minefield, no clear use case)
- Auto-fix suggestions (this plugin diagnoses; coding plugins prescribe)
- Web dashboard (JSON + markdown is enough for v1)
- Sub-agent fan-out per surface (sequential single-process is fine until profiling proves otherwise)

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

Introduces `@elizaos/plugin-gitpathologist`, a new optional plugin that performs forensic git-history analysis for a path/glob surface, producing a scored health timeline, peak/drift inflection detection, and an LLM-narrated (or heuristic-fallback) rot post-mortem. It wires into `OPTIONAL_CORE_PLUGINS` and auto-loads when the workspace contains a `.git` directory.

- **Pipeline:** deterministic spine (`scan` → `classify` → `score` → `inflect`) with LLM only in the `narrate` phase, capped by a configurable `budget`; all commit text and diff snippets pass through a local secret scrubber before leaving the process.
- **Integration:** `gitpathologist` boolean added to `AgentEntryConfig`, `AgentDefaultsConfig`, and both Zod schemas; a `gitpathologistRequested()` gate respects config → env-var → auto-detect precedence and excludes mobile platforms.
- **Cache:** reports stored as JSON under `<repo>/.milady/gitpathology/`; `read()` and `list()` still call `JSON.parse` without a try/catch (flagged in prior review round).

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

Safe to merge once the unguarded JSON.parse calls in the cache layer are addressed; the rest of the pipeline is well-structured and self-contained.

The cache's read() and list() methods still call JSON.parse without a try/catch, meaning a single corrupted cache file throws through runReport() and crashes the analysis instead of falling back gracefully.

plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/cache/report-cache.ts — unguarded JSON.parse in both read() and list() is the outstanding concern from the prior review round.

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/pipeline/scan.ts | Deterministic git-log parser. runGit correctly checks result.error. findFileBlockStart has an edge case where tab-delimited numeric body lines are misidentified as numstat output. |
| plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/pipeline/score.ts | EMA health scorer with revert-proximity penalty. byShortSha uses 7-char SHA prefixes; a collision could silently apply the revert penalty to the wrong commit. |
| plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/pipeline/narrate.ts | LLM narration phase with budget guard. Budget correctly caps only LLM calls; every drift always gets a RotCause. Secret scrubbing applied before model calls. |
| plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/pipeline/inflect.ts | Peak/drift detection with deduplication. The tiedEarlier check correctly uses findIndex on points. |

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Agent as Agent Runtime
    participant Action as GIT_PATHOLOGY Action
    participant Svc as GitPathologyService
    participant Cache as ReportCache
    participant Git as git (spawnSync)
    participant LLM as useModel (LLM)

    Agent->>Action: "handler(operation=report, surface, opts)"
    Action->>Svc: runReport(surface, overrides)
    Svc->>Git: rev-parse HEAD
    Svc->>Cache: read(cacheKey)
    alt Cache hit + HEAD matches
        Cache-->>Svc: PathologyReport
    else Cache miss or HEAD changed
        Svc->>Git: git log --numstat (scan)
        Git-->>Svc: RawCommit[]
        Svc->>Svc: classify to score to inflect
        loop each drift up to budget LLM calls
            Svc->>Git: git show diff snippet
            Svc->>LLM: buildPrompt useModel
            LLM-->>Svc: RotCause or fallback
        end
        Svc->>Cache: write(PathologyReport)
    end
    Svc-->>Action: PathologyReport
    Action-->>Agent: ActionResult text data
```

1. `plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/cache/report-cache.ts`, line 52-57 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/a98ea39869976595088b43d89075ef4902b7d16c/plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/cache/report-cache.ts#L52-L57)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> Both `read()` and `list()` call `JSON.parse()` without a try/catch. A partially-written or corrupted cache file will throw a `SyntaxError` that bubbles up through `runReport()` uncaught, crashing the entire analysis pipeline instead of treating the bad entry as a cache miss.

2. `plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/cache/report-cache.ts`, line 69-71 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/a98ea39869976595088b43d89075ef4902b7d16c/plugins/plugin-gitpathologist/src/cache/report-cache.ts#L69-L71))

Any malformed file in the cache directory will throw and prevent the full listing from being returned. Skipping bad entries is the right recovery.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->c:T1fcc,## what

four chunks of desktop ux that go together because they all share the home/pill plumbing:

1. **clouds/avatar home as the post-onboarding default.** `HomeView` renders the clouds backdrop + centered voice waveform when the assistant is idle, and the typed prose + composer chrome stays inside the home surface (no extra app chrome when the OS overlay is on). `navigation/main-tab.ts` falls back to home when no plugin app declares `elizaos.app.mainTab=true`.

2. **opt-in OS pill (`ELIZA_DESKTOP_PILL=1`).** `electrobun/src/index.ts` + `pill-window.ts` spawn a separate transparent OS-level toplevel that loads the renderer with `?shellMode=chat-overlay`. the pill is gated off kiosk mode and stays opt-in until dynamic native frame sizing lands (the current native rectangle is fixed 360×280 and risks invisible click interception when always-on-top, per shaw).

3. **chat composer visibility.** `chat-composer.tsx` + `chat-composer-shell.tsx` add a real surface (border + bg color-mix + inset shadow + backdrop-blur) so the composer is visible against the dark chat backdrop. previously the composer was invisible on dark mode.

4. **startup coordinator auto-recovery.** `useStartupCoordinator.ts` adds `recoverTerminalStartupError()` that polls `/api/status` + `/api/onboarding/status` every 2.5s when the coordinator is stuck in a recoverable error state (backend-timeout, backend-unreachable, agent-timeout, agent-error, unknown). when the agent later comes back, the coordinator transitions to running without requiring a manual retry. also adds a `storage` event listener so a separate chat-overlay pill window can notice when onboarding completes in the main app.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->d:Tb7d,## what

three things on the local-inference path:

1. **dflash binary discovery from default user state.** `dflash-server.ts` walks both `ELIZA_STATE_DIR` (isolated app-run state) AND the default user root (`~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/`). preserves backend preference ordering (cuda > vulkan > rocm > metal > cpu) and picks the durable build over older fused/test builds. fixes the case where the runtime state is isolated per-app but the dflash binary lives in the default user dir.

2. **runtime router wiring in `packages/agent`.** `eliza.ts` adds `ensureLocalInferenceRuntimeHandlers()` which lazy-imports `@elizaos/plugin-local-inference/runtime` and registers the local handlers (TEXT_SMALL, TEXT_LARGE, TEXT_EMBEDDING, RESPONSE_HANDLER, ACTION_PLANNER, etc.) into the runtime instead of relying on static `Plugin.models` exports. `plugin-collector.ts` drops the `ELIZA_DISABLE_LOCAL_EMBEDDINGS=1`-removes-the-whole-plugin code path — the embedding handler now self-disables instead of yanking the whole plugin.

3. **dflash eval gating.** `manifest/validator.ts` requires a passing `evals.dflash` block when a manifest is both `defaultEligible: true` AND ships `files.dflash`. needs-hardware bench (null acceptanceRate or speedup) cannot be marked `passed: true`. matches the pattern used for asr / embedMteb / vadLatency. `manifest/schema.ts` keeps dflash as a per-tier required kernel.

also adds `dflash` to `catalog.ts` BASE_REQUIRED_KERNELS so the runtime probe matches the manifest contract.

## windows ffi build path proven (build-time fixes split into #7988)

after merging recent upstream, the desktop FFI backend requires `libllama.dll` + `libeliza-llama-shim.dll` built via `packages/app-core/scripts/build-llama-cpp-desktop-dylib.mjs` and staged at `~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/llama-cpp/windows-x86_64-<backend>/`.

two windows build-time gaps surfaced and are fixed in #7988:

- `build-llama-cpp-desktop-dylib.mjs` now finds `llama.dll` on win32 (msvc drops the `lib` prefix) and stages it as `libllama.dll` so `desktop-llama-adapter.ts` resolves it.
- `eliza_llama_shim.c` now defines five symbols that were declared in the header and called by the adapter but had no body: `context_detach_drafter`, `context_has_drafter`, `dflash_stats_ex`, `context_handle_memory_pressure`, and the `mtp_stats` adapter alias. msvc was refusing to link the shim with `LNK2019`.

verified end-to-end on windows-x86_64 cuda (visual studio 17 2022 + cuda 12.4 + ninja): shim loaded into `bun:ffi` with all 39 symbols resolved, runtime reached the prewarm path on the same code path linux/macos hit.

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR applies five targeted fixes to break a circular-import TDZ cascade on Bun's strict-ESM evaluator that was causing the elizaOS live USB agent to die at startup. The core fix converts the static `@elizaos/agent` import in `vault-bootstrap.ts` to a lazy dynamic import via `agentBridge()`, and hardens three module-level memoization slots (`cachedManager`, `cache`, `bridgeCache`) against re-entry before their initializers run by switching from `let`/`const` to `var`-wrapped container objects.

- **`vault-bootstrap.ts`**: Agent imports converted to type-only + lazy `agentBridge()` dynamic import; all downstream helpers now receive a `bridge` parameter injected from the single resolution point at `runVaultBootstrap`.
- **`vault-mirror.ts` / `registry/index.ts`**: `let` cache slots replaced with `var`-wrapped containers plus self-heal guards; `_resetSharedVaultForTesting` accesses `state.manager` directly without the same guard added to `sharedSecretsManager()`.
- **`cloud-provisioning.ts`**: Stale re-export from `@elizaos/shared` replaced with a local function definition; the logic is currently byte-for-byte identical to the shared implementation, so there is no behavioral divergence at this time.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

Safe to merge; the five targeted changes address a real startup crash with no new dependencies, no unsafe casts, and no behavioral changes outside the circular-import boot path.

All changed files contain narrowly scoped, well-documented fixes: the dynamic import bridge, the var-wrapped container pattern, and the const-to-function-getter conversions are all mechanically correct. The isCloudProvisionedContainer local copy is byte-for-byte identical to the shared source at this revision. Previously flagged concerns are acknowledged trade-offs and do not affect production boot correctness.

No files require special attention beyond the previously discussed items.

- Root-cause diagnosis of the 8-day nightly failure
- Two fix options (single-job vs split prep-app + build-iso)
- Industry-standard practices vs where we are (Debian/Fedora/Arch/Tails comparison)
- Priority-ordered plan: unstick nightly → fix release-tag path → verify gh attestation → extend to true multi-arch → signed flasher
- Open questions on GPG key ownership, mirrors, channel cadence, hardware support matrix

<!-- /greptile_comment -->12:T15a1,## Why

Natural follow-up to PR #7976 (which made the GPG secrets optional in \`publish-apt-repo.yml\` so the release workflow stops startup_failing). That PR makes the workflow tolerant of missing secrets — **this doc tells the maintainer how to provide them when they're ready to flip publishing on**.

## The change (1 file, +200 lines, docs-only)

\`packages/os/docs/admin-apt-repo-setup.md\` walks through:

1. **Generate a dedicated GPG signing key.** Don't reuse a personal key — CI runs unattended, revocation should be scoped.
2. **Export the ASCII-armored private key** for CI ingestion (with secure handling instructions: shred after paste).
3. **Set the three GitHub secrets** (\`DEBIAN_GPG_PRIVATE_KEY\`, \`DEBIAN_GPG_KEY_ID\`, optional \`DEBIAN_GPG_PASSPHRASE\`) — repo OR org level both work, with the exact URL.
4. **Sanity-trigger the workflow** via \`gh workflow run\` to confirm wiring before tagging a release.
5. **Publish the matching public key** at a stable URL + concrete end-user \`apt\` install commands they can copy-paste from your release notes.

Plus two playbooks:

- **Key rotation** (default 2-year expiry from Step 1) — sign new key with old key for transition trust.
- **Revocation** — compromised-key recovery via \`gpg --gen-revoke\` + publishing the revoked public key.

## Why this shape

- **Maintainer-facing** — the audience is someone with repo admin access, not an end user. Different tone + concrete URLs to the actual settings pages.
- **Pairs with** [verify-iso-download.md](packages/os/docs/verify-iso-download.md) (PR #7975) — that one is for ISO download verifiers, this one is for the admins shipping the apt repo end of the same pipeline.
- **Doc-only**, no code change. Lowest-risk merge.

## Related

- #7976 — secrets-optional fix (this PR's prerequisite)
- #7975 — end-user verify-ISO docs (pair)
- [ci-cd-prod-roadmap.md](packages/os/docs/ci-cd-prod-roadmap.md) (PR #7949) — broader release-pipeline status
- Documented in HANDOFF_2026-05-25 memory

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds a new maintainer playbook (`admin-apt-repo-setup.md`) that walks through the one-time process of configuring GPG secrets to enable signed apt-repo publishing via the `publish-apt-repo.yml` workflow introduced in PR #7976. It is a docs-only change with no code modifications.

- **Key rotation section has a backwards description**: the prose says "Sign the old key with the new key" but for apt trust transitions the correct operation is signing the new key with the old key — the accompanying `gpg --sign-key` command happens to be correct, but the description will mislead a maintainer trying to understand the intent.
- **Round-trip verification step is misleading**: importing a key back into the same keyring where it was just generated will report "already in secret keyring", not the expected "secret key imported"; `gpg --show-keys` is the right check here.
- **End-user install snippet uses the deprecated `trusted.gpg.d/.asc` pattern** instead of the modern `/etc/apt/keyrings/` + `signed-by=` approach, which silently fails on Ubuntu 20.04 LTS systems.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

Safe to merge as a documentation addition, but three inaccuracies in the playbook should be corrected before maintainers act on it.

The key rotation section describes the cross-signing direction backwards — a maintainer following the description literally would sign in the wrong direction, breaking the trust chain for existing apt users. The round-trip verification step will silently succeed without actually proving the exported file is valid. Together these two issues could cause a real operational mistake during key rotation or initial setup. The trusted.gpg.d/.asc pattern is an additional correctness concern for users on older LTS systems.

packages/os/docs/admin-apt-repo-setup.md — the key rotation prose, verification command, and end-user install snippet all need correction.

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>

| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/os/docs/admin-apt-repo-setup.md | New admin playbook for GPG-signed apt-repo setup; contains a backwards cross-signing description in the key rotation section, a misleading round-trip verification step, and end-user install instructions that use the deprecated trusted.gpg.d/.asc pattern instead of the modern signed-by approach. |

</details>

<h3>Flowchart</h3>

```mermaid
%%{init: {'theme': 'neutral'}}%%
flowchart TD
    A[Step 1: Generate dedicated GPG key] --> B[Step 2: Export private key]
    B --> C{Verify exported file with gpg --show-keys}
    C -->|valid| D[shred /tmp/elizaos-apt-private.asc]
    C -->|invalid| B
    D --> E[Step 3: Set GitHub Secrets]
    E --> F[Step 4: Sanity-trigger workflow]
    F --> G{Check GPG credentials step}
    G -->|can_publish=true| H[Builds signed .deb, pushes to apt-repo branch]
    G -->|warning: secret missing| E
    H --> I[Step 5: Export + commit public key]
    I --> J[Publish end-user install instructions]
    subgraph Maintenance
        K[Key Rotation: sign new key with old key]
        L[Revocation: gpg --gen-revoke, publish revoked cert]
    end
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->13:T175a,## Why

When users download an elizaOS Live ISO from GitHub Releases they need a single canonical guide for verifying provenance + integrity before flashing. Today the verify instructions are scattered across the CI/CD roadmap doc, action README comments, and Sigstore upstream — users with no security background hit a wall.

## The change (1 file, +153 / -0)

New \`packages/os/docs/verify-iso-download.md\` covers:

- **What every release ships** — \`.iso\` + \`.iso.sha256\` + \`.iso.spdx.json\` + the GitHub-OIDC Sigstore attestation (no separate file needed)
- **Three-step verify**, each with exact commands + expected output:
  1. \`sha256sum -c\` for integrity
  2. \`gh attestation verify --owner elizaOS\` for SLSA provenance
  3. Optional \`syft\` / \`grype\` SBOM scan for CVE coverage
- **Hardened verify** with \`--source-ref\` + \`--signer-workflow\` for security-sensitive deployments (rejects forks, rejects different workflows)
- **Offline verify** path via \`gh attestation download\` then \`gh attestation verify --bundle\` (useful when flashing on an air-gapped machine)
- **Honest disclosure** of what verification does NOT guarantee (GitHub Actions / Sigstore being uncompromised, app-layer bugs)
- **Failure-mode → fix table** for the common cases (release predates the SLSA pipeline, wrong owner, fork download, attestation exists but workflow mismatch, etc.)

## Why this shape

- **One canonical doc.** No more "where do I copy the verify command from" — link to one URL from release notes / README / Twitter / Discord.
- **Honest about scope.** The "what verification does NOT guarantee" + "when verification fails" sections set the right trust model up front.
- **Future-proof.** Works for amd64 today, arm64 once Phase 3a (#7971) lands, riscv64 when Shaw's enablement gets there. No arch-specific instructions needed.

## Related

- [ci-cd-prod-roadmap.md](packages/os/docs/ci-cd-prod-roadmap.md) (PR #7949) — why these attestations don't yet ship on every release (nightly red 8+ days, PR #7950 unblocks)
- [.github/workflows/build-linux-iso.yml](.github/workflows/build-linux-iso.yml) — the workflow that mints the SLSA attestation (PR #7950 + PR #7973 wire it correctly)
- Documented in HANDOFF_2026-05-25 memory

This PR adds a new end-user guide (`packages/os/docs/verify-iso-download.md`) for verifying elizaOS Live ISO downloads using SHA-256 checksums, `gh attestation verify` (SLSA provenance via Sigstore/GitHub OIDC), and optional SBOM scanning with Grype. <!-- /greptile_comment -->14:T1d92,## Summary

When Stage 1 asserts `requiresTool=true` but no exposed tool can fulfill the request (e.g. chat-history search with no `SEARCH_MESSAGES` action), the planner emits valid honest REPLY refusals each iteration. The loop previously discarded every REPLY, hit `maxRequiredToolMisses`, threw `TrajectoryLimitExceeded`, and the caller surfaced a generic apology ("Sorry, something went wrong—please try again") instead of the planner's real answer.

Live trajectory `tj-3bb6dc66be0c16.json` (probe "can you go check the history and tell me what was said about ironpike99") ran 4 iterations at \$0.087, each emitting a valid REPLY refusal, all discarded, user got the generic error.

## Fix

- Add `lastTerminalRefusalText` tracker in the planner loop.
- In both gated branches (planner emits 0 toolCalls + planner emits only-terminal toolCalls), capture the most recent refusal via `getNonEmptyString(plannerOutput.messageToUser)` and `terminalMessageFromToolCalls(...)`.
- Before throwing `TrajectoryLimitExceeded`, if `requiredToolMisses >= config.maxRequiredToolMisses` AND a refusal was captured, return via new helper `finishWithCapturedRefusal` which pushes a terminal step and returns the captured text as \`finalMessage\`.
- Defensive: cap still throws when the planner produces no usable text across all iterations, so the caller can still fall back to the generic apology path when there is genuinely nothing to surface.

## Tests

- \`planner-loop-tools-vs-schema.test.ts\`: rewrote "caps required-tool planner misses" to assert the new contract (returns the captured refusal as finalMessage); added a sibling test that asserts the cap still throws when no usable text is captured.
- \`planner-loop.test.ts\`: added regression covering the REPLY-tool branch specifically (two iterations of REPLY refusals, cap hits on the second, the captured REPLY text becomes the final message).

## Test plan

- [x] Local unit tests pass (45/45 across both planner-loop test files)
- [x] Full runtime suite passes (689/689, 2 skip, 0 fail)
- [x] Live verification on milady deployment: probe shapes that previously hit "Sorry, something went wrong" now return the planner's honest refusal text

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR fixes a UX regression where the planner loop discarded valid REPLY refusals, hit `maxRequiredToolMisses`, threw `TrajectoryLimitExceeded`, and the caller surfaced a generic apology instead of the planner's honest answer. Alongside the core loop fix, it tightens planner prompt rules (SHELL/TASKS_SPAWN_AGENT misuse, dead-hint echo-placeholder prohibition) and adds a personal-crisis deferral rule to the message-handler template.

- **Core fix (`planner-loop.ts`)**: Adds a `lastTerminalRefusalText` tracker and `finishWithCapturedRefusal` helper; when the required-tool cap fires and a non-empty refusal was captured across iterations, the loop returns it as `finalMessage` instead of throwing. <!-- /greptile_comment -->15:T26b5,## The bug (reproduced 2026-05-25 in QEMU live ISO)

Click **SIGN IN WITH ELIZA CLOUD** → button changes to "WAITING FOR AUTH..." → **the user is stuck**. No browser opens, no URL is shown, no error appears. Agent IS reaching Cloud correctly (\`login_create_session\` returns 201, polling \`login_poll_status\` HTTP 200 every 1s), but there's no path for the user to actually authenticate.

## Root cause

Renderer flow today:
1. \`handleCloudLogin\` calls \`POST /api/cloud/login\` → gets \`{sessionId, browserUrl: \"https://www.elizacloud.ai/auth/cli-login?session=<uuid>\"}\`
2. Calls \`openExternalUrl(browserUrl)\` which tries Capacitor → Electrobun \`desktopOpenExternal\` → \`window.open\`
3. If the call throws, sets \`elizaCloudLoginError\` to \"Open this link to log in: <url>\" — visible fallback
4. **If the call resolves OK but the browser never actually surfaces a window, the user sees nothing.**

Step 4 is the gap. In Tails, \`xdg-open\` routes through \`gtk-launch\` to the Tor Browser flatpak. The flatpak spawn succeeds (returns \"ok\"), no exception is thrown, but the browser window can hang behind the scenes — Tor never bootstraps in NAT environments, the flatpak is mid-startup, the WM doesn't raise it, etc.

Every OAuth-device-code CLI (gh auth login, npm login, stripe login) handles this by **always printing the verification URL + code**: the auto-open is a bonus, the manual link is the contract.

## Fix (5 files, +160 lines)

### 1. \`useCloudState\` exposes a new \`elizaCloudLoginFallbackUrl\` state
- Set unconditionally when \`resp.browserUrl\` arrives (not only on openExternalUrl failure)
- Cleared in \`stopCloudLoginPolling\` (authenticated / errored / timed out / cancelled) and in the outer catch
- Added to ambient types: \`types.ts\`, \`types.d.ts\`, \`useCloudState.d.ts\`

### 2. \`RuntimeGate\` renders a \`CloudLoginFallbackPanel\` below the spinning sign-in button
Shown only when \`elizaCloudLoginBusy && elizaCloudLoginFallbackUrl\` are both true. Contents:
- Prompt: \"Sign-in window didn't open? Visit this link on any device:\"
- URL in a \`select-all\` monospace block (manual copy always works)
- \"Open link\" button → retries \`openExternalUrl(browserUrl)\` (fresh user-gesture often succeeds where the first auto-open silently failed)
- \"Copy\" button → \`copyTextToClipboard\` with 2s \"Copied\" confirmation; gracefully degrades to the select-all block if clipboard is unavailable

### 3. Test fixture in \`RuntimeGate.cloud-provisioning.test.tsx\` gets the new field
So type-check stays green for existing tests.

## Why this shape

- **Standard device-code UX.** Matches the contract every other CLI/desktop OAuth flow honors.
- **No regression on happy path.** Panel only shows during an active login + after the URL is known. Users on platforms where auto-open works see the panel briefly before they sign in on the other tab — no friction added.
- **Multiple recovery vectors.** Auto-open + retry-click + manual copy + select-all-text. At least one works in every environment.
- **i18n-ready.** Uses the existing \`t()\` helper with \`defaultValue\` fallbacks. New keys: \`runtimegate.cloudFallbackPrompt\`, \`runtimegate.cloudFallbackOpen\`, \`runtimegate.cloudFallbackCopy\`, \`runtimegate.cloudFallbackCopied\`.
- **Type-clean.** \`bunx tsc --noEmit\` shows zero new errors in changed files. \`biome check\` clean on 5 changed files.

## Test plan

- [x] \`tsc --noEmit\` clean on changed files
- [x] \`biome check\` clean on changed files
- [ ] In the live ISO: click SIGN IN WITH ELIZA CLOUD → verify panel appears with copyable URL + working \"Open link\" / \"Copy\" buttons
- [ ] On macOS / Windows desktop where openExternalUrl works correctly: verify the auto-open still fires AND the panel is also visible as confirmation
- [ ] Verify panel disappears when user cancels (Back) or signs in successfully

- Part of live-ISO bug triage with PR #7958 (docs flatpak fix) and PR #7966 (persistence-maintenance trap fix).
- The wider TDZ root fix is in PR #7947.

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds a device-code-style fallback panel to the Eliza Cloud sign-in flow, always displaying the verification URL alongside the spinner so users on environments where `xdg-open` silently fails (e.g. Tails routing through the Tor Browser flatpak) have a reliable path to complete authentication.

<h3>Flowchart</h3>

```mermaid
%%{init: {'theme': 'neutral'}}%%
flowchart TD
    A[User runs verify-release.sh DIR] --> B{SHA256SUMS present?}
    B -- No --> C[fail + exit 1]
    B -- Yes --> D{sha256sum / shasum available?}
    D -- No --> C
    D -- Yes --> E{Roundtrip OK?}
    E -- No --> C
    E -- Yes --> F[ok: Layer 1 passed]
    F --> G{gh CLI installed?}
    G -- No --> H[warn: skip attestations]
    G -- Yes --> I[gh attestation verify each artifact]
    I --> J{mixed pass/fail?}
    J -- Yes --> K[fail + EXIT=2]
    J -- No all-fail --> L[warn: no attestations verified]
    J -- all-pass --> M[ok: attestations clean]
    H & K & L & M --> N
    N{SHA256SUMS.asc / .sig present?}
    N -- No --> O[warn: skip GPG]
    N -- Yes --> P{gpg installed?}
    P -- No --> Q[warn: skip GPG]
    P -- Yes --> R{Good signature?}
    R -- No --> S[fail + EXIT=2]
    R -- Yes --> T[ok: GPG verified]
    O & Q & S & T --> U
    U{*.spdx.json present?}
    U -- No --> V[warn: skip SBOM]
    U -- Yes --> W{jq installed?}
    W -- No --> X[warn: skip SBOM]
    W -- Yes --> Y[ok: package count per SBOM]
    V & X & Y --> Z[exit EXIT]
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->17:T240c,Closes #7967.

## Summary

A single user prompt fans out into multiple sub-agent sessions when the orchestrator auto-respawns (on state_lost, verify-retry, transient error, blocked) — and **every** sub-agent's \`task_complete\` event posts a separate user-facing Discord message. The user sees 2-3+ overlapping replies in 30 seconds with junk URLs, duplicates, or page sub-resources from each retry.

## Repro on 2026-05-25 07:05-07:06 UTC

Prompt: \`@bot what is the current stable python version\`

Parent emitted **one** \`TASKS:spawn_agent\` call. Sub-agent trajectories fired:
- \`tj-f3e500c94869d9\` — blocked
- \`tj-f3e1bdae0bf33b\` — error ("state was lost")
- \`tj-f4348d0432b057\` — task_complete (python.org URLs)
- \`tj-f4823d19a147b7\` — task_complete (**analytics.python.org/js/script.file-downloads.outbound-links.js** — page sub-resource leak)
- \`tj-f4b3d723dc9ee1\` — task_complete (python.org URLs)

User saw three Discord messages:
1. "I don't have live access to retrieve the latest stable Python version number right now. https://www.python.org/downloads/"
2. "https://analytics.python.org/js/script.file-downloads.outbound-links.js"
3. "https://www.python.org/downloads/"

## Fix

Track \`(originMessageId → first sessionId that posted task_complete)\` for up to 1024 origin messages (LRU FIFO drop). When a later task_complete event arrives for the SAME \`originMessageId\` from a DIFFERENT \`sessionId\`, absorb it silently with a debug log.

**Same-session progressive task_completes are unaffected** — the dedup check compares sessionIds. The existing test \`does NOT dedup task_complete with a different response\` continues to pass.

## Tests

- New regression \`absorbs cross-session task_complete for the same origin message (cascade-retry dedup)\` — two sessions sharing one originMessageId, emits task_complete for each, asserts only the first reaches handleMessage.
- All 59/59 sub-agent-router tests pass.

## Open follow-ups (not in this PR)

- Sub-agent task-report cleaning to drop tracker/analytics URLs at source.
- Investigate **WHY** the orchestrator spawns 5 sessions per prompt — the cascade is the symptom; the root respawn loop is the underlying defect.

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR addresses the duplicate-reply cascade (issue #7967) where a single user prompt fans out into multiple sub-agent sessions — each firing its own `task_complete` — by adding an origin-message dedup map in `SubAgentRouter`. It also bundles several related runtime improvements: raising the ACP timeout from 30 s to 300 s, redacting loopback URLs from outgoing text, surfacing honest planner refusals instead of generic apologies, preventing bare-ack simple-path replies, and fixing evaluator JSON envelope leakage.

- **Dedup (`sub-agent-router.ts`)**: tracks the first session that successfully posts `task_complete` for each origin message; later sessions for the same origin are silently suppressed. The map is bounded at 1 024 entries with FIFO eviction.
- **Timeout (`acp-native-transport.ts`)**: `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` raised from 30 s to 300 s with env-var override support, stopping the retry storm that generated 44 sub-agent trajectories per prompt.
- **Planner / evaluator fixes**: three new prompt rules guard against SHELL/TASKS fallbacks for chat-recall, `stripTrailingEvaluatorEnvelope` prevents the evaluator's JSON object from leaking into Discord, and `candidateActions` non-emptiness now triggers planner routing even when `requiresTool=false`.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

The dedup guard at the heart of this fix has a concurrency window that allows the exact duplicate posts it is meant to prevent when two task_complete events arrive before either handler completes its async work.

The `originHasPostedFromOtherSession` check and the `markOriginCompletionPosted` call are separated by several awaited async operations (URL verification via HTTP, `retryIncompleteBuild`, `handleMessage`). The event handler is fire-and-forget — two concurrent `task_complete` events both enter `handleEvent` before either reaches the mark step, so both see an empty map and both post to the user. The test serialises events with `setImmediate` between each one, so it cannot catch this window. On a busy deployment where the orchestrator fans out multiple sessions simultaneously (the exact scenario described in the PR), this race is likely to reproduce.

The concurrency gap is in `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/sub-agent-router.ts`, specifically the ordering and placement of the dedup check relative to the async URL verification and retry-spawn logic.

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/sub-agent-router.ts | Core fix: adds origin-message dedup map and `redactLoopbackUrls`. | plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/acp-native-transport.ts | Raises default ACP timeout from 30 s to 300 s with clear rationale; test verifies the new 5-minute boundary. Straightforward and well-documented. |
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/actions/tasks.ts | Removes routing-kind label leakage from sub-agent prompt instructions; test verifies the new wording. Low risk change. |
| packages/core/src/runtime/evaluator.ts | Adds `stripTrailingEvaluatorEnvelope` to avoid the evaluator's JSON object leaking into Discord. The `"success"` key used as a heuristic can match common API-response JSON and may produce false-positive strips. |
| packages/core/src/runtime/message-handler.ts | Routes to planner when `candidateActions` is non-empty even with `requiresTool=false`; prevents "On it." bare-ack disappearances. Tests cover both promotion and safe-path cases. |

Any user-facing answer that ends with a JSON object containing `"success":` — a realistic code or API explanation — will have that trailing object stripped before it reaches the user. A safer guard would require at least two evaluator-specific keys to be present (e.g., both `"success"` and `"decision"`), since the combination is far more distinctive than either alone.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->19:T1e29,## what

three small core changes that go together:

1. **`parseToonKeyValue` in `utils.ts`** — generated-plugin prompts sometimes return TOON-shape (\`key: value\` with optional indexed arrays \`items[0]: value\`) inside a markdown fence. add a tolerant parser that strips the fence and walks the lines, preserving JSON-literal values verbatim so large IDs (discord snowflakes) don't get clipped by Number. exported from the `node` / `edge` / `browser` index barrels.

2. **`StructuredResponseFormat` includes `\"TOON\"`** — `runtime.ts` + `types/state.ts`. lets generated plugins declare their structured response shape as TOON and have the failure-mode error reporter understand it (no more `format: \"JSON\"` lie when the candidate was TOON).

3. **`evaluator` + `planner-loop` pin thinking=off, maxTokens=1024.** evaluation + planning are short structured calls; they should not burn budget on extended reasoning. add `providerOptions.eliza.thinking = \"off\"` and `maxTokens = 1024` to both call sites. `planner-types.ts` adds `maxTokens` to the model param signature.

evaluator + planner-loop: unchanged (regression coverage already there)

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR makes three coordinated additions to `@elizaos/core`: a tolerant TOON key-value parser (`parseToonKeyValue`) exported from all index barrels, a `"TOON"` variant added to `StructuredResponseFormat` and `StructuredOutputFailure.format`, and `thinking: "off"` + `maxTokens: 1024` pinned on both the evaluator and planner-loop call sites.

- **`parseToonKeyValue`** walks `key: value` / `items[0]: value` lines from a markdown fence, preserving large numeric IDs as strings to avoid 53-bit float precision loss. Boolean literals (`true`/`false`) are returned as strings, inconsistent with `null` which correctly converts to JS `null`.
- **`StructuredResponseFormat` + `StructuredOutputFailure.format`** are widened to include `"TOON"`, but `resolveDefaultOutputFormat` and the extraction pipeline in `runtime.ts` still never produce `"TOON"` at runtime (flagged separately).
- **Evaluator** correctly pins `maxTokens: 1024` (only emits a short decision); **planner-loop** applies the same cap despite requiring complete JSON tool-call output that can exceed 1024 tokens (flagged separately).

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

The TOON parser and evaluator changes are safe to merge; the planner and runtime.ts changes carry unresolved correctness gaps that should be reviewed before merging.

Two issues flagged in prior reviews remain unaddressed: the planner's 1024-token cap can truncate mid-JSON on tool calls that carry user-facing text, and resolveDefaultOutputFormat still maps 'toon' to 'JSON', meaning the TOON error-reporting path is dead code. The new parseToonKeyValue and evaluator changes are low-risk additions.

packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts and packages/core/src/runtime.ts need the most attention — the former for the token cap on open-ended tool output, the latter for the incomplete TOON format routing.

<sub>Reviews (3): Last reviewed commit: ["core: add toon key-value parser + force ..."](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/commit/32a1fd8e0ef0adab3e930c6bcb1ebd9c287a8274) | [Re-trigger Greptile](https://app.greptile.com/api/retrigger?id=33797493)</sub>

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1a:T282e,## Phase 3a — multi-arch enablement

Three coordinated changes drive the target architecture from a single \`ELIZAOS_ARCH\` env var, defaulted to amd64 so every existing call site keeps working unchanged.

### 1. \`build.sh\`
- Validate \`ELIZAOS_ARCH\` (\`amd64\` | \`arm64\` | \`riscv64\`)
- Tag the builder image per-arch (\`elizaos-builder-\${ARCH}\`) so concurrent per-arch builds don't race
- Pass \`--platform linux/\${ARCH}\` + \`--build-arg TARGETARCH=\${ARCH}\` to \`docker build\`
- Forward \`ELIZAOS_ARCH\` into the container so \`tails/auto/config\` can drive \`lb config\` off it

### 2. \`Dockerfile\`
- New \`ARG TARGETARCH=amd64\`
- Split apt install into a common-deps RUN (debootstrap, cpio, xorriso, grub2-common, …) and a per-arch bootloader RUN:
  - **amd64**: \`isolinux syslinux syslinux-common grub-pc-bin grub-efi-amd64-bin\` (full Tails BIOS+UEFI stack)
  - **arm64**: \`grub-efi-arm64-bin\` (UEFI-only; arm64 has no BIOS)
  - **riscv64**: \`grub-efi-riscv64-bin\` (Debian trixie's riscv64 port ships it)
- \`isolinux + syslinux + grub-pc-bin\` are x86-only — installing them on arm64/riscv64 errors with "Unable to locate package"

### 3. \`tails/auto/config\`
- Read + validate \`ELIZAOS_ARCH\`
- Use it in \`BUILD_BASENAME\` (\`tails-\${ARCH}-…iso\`)
- Change the \`dpkg --print-architecture\` sanity check to compare with \`ELIZAOS_ARCH\` (so the container-arch mismatch shows the actual problem instead of "Only amd64 is supported")
- Feed \`--architecture \${ARCH}\` and \`--linux-flavours \${ARCH}\` to \`lb config\`
- Conditionalize the \`--syslinux-*\` args (dropped on arm64/riscv64 because \`lb config\` otherwise tries to install syslinux during build and fails)

## Backwards-compatibility

Existing \`./build.sh\` (no env var) defaults to amd64 and produces the same artifact path as before: \`tails-amd64-…iso\`. Zero regression for current CI / nightly / dev workflows.

## What this does NOT do (next steps)

- **Run a fresh arm64 build end-to-end**. Needs \`qemu-user-static + binfmt_misc\` on the host. On bare Linux: \`apt-get install qemu-user-static binfmt-support && docker run --rm --privileged tonistiigi/binfmt --install all\`. Docker Desktop bundles it. Several hours of emulated build time the first run. Once it completes, \`qemu-system-aarch64 -M virt -bios QEMU_EFI.fd\` should boot the ISO.
- **Add arm64 to \`.github/workflows/build-linux-iso.yml\`'s matrix**. Should be \`continue-on-error: true\` initially so amd64 nightly stays green while arm64 stabilizes. Gated on local arm64 build proving the parameterization works end-to-end.
- **riscv64 userspace**. The OS-level multi-arch path is now ready, but the userspace apps still need their own arch-specific binaries — Shaw's electrobun-riscv64 enablement patches (currently only diagnostic instrumentation) + an 8-hour Bun cross-build at \`packages/app-core/scripts/bun-riscv64/\` are both pre-reqs for a usable riscv64 ISO.

## Verification

- [x] \`bash -n\` clean on build.sh; \`sh -n\` clean on \`tails/auto/config\`
- [x] Per-arch smoke: \`ELIZAOS_ARCH=arm64 build.sh\` sets \`IMAGE=elizaos-builder-arm64\`, \`ELIZAOS_ARCH=bogus\` is rejected before \`docker build\`
- [x] Dockerfile parses cleanly; the per-arch case statement installs the correct bootloader package set
- [ ] End-to-end arm64 build on a host with qemu-user-static (next session)
- [ ] arm64 ISO boot test in qemu-system-aarch64

## Related

- Part of the Phase 2 → Phase 3 plan in HANDOFF_2026-05-25 (memory)
- Phase 2 amd64 ISO is verified working end-to-end; PRs #7947, #7949, #7950, #7958, #7966, #7968 cover the boot fixes + CI fixes + UX fixes already shipped
- Phase 3b riscv64 follow-up depends on Shaw's electrobun-riscv64 patches landing

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR parameterizes the elizaOS ISO builder to support `amd64`, `arm64`, and `riscv64` via a single `ELIZAOS_ARCH` env var that defaults to `amd64`, keeping all existing call sites unchanged.

- **`build.sh`**: validates `ELIZAOS_ARCH`, uses per-arch image tags (`elizaos-builder-${ARCH}`), and forwards the arch into `docker build` (`--platform`, `--build-arg TARGETARCH`) and `docker run` (`-e ELIZAOS_ARCH`).
- **`Dockerfile`**: adds `ARG TARGETARCH=amd64` with an early validation `RUN`, splits the monolithic apt install into a common-deps layer and a per-arch bootloader layer (x86-only `isolinux`/`syslinux`/`grub-pc-bin` for amd64; EFI-only `grub-efi-<arch>-bin` for arm64/riscv64).
- **`tails/auto/config`**: validates `ELIZAOS_ARCH`, threads it into `BUILD_BASENAME`, `lb config --architecture` / `--linux-flavours`, the `dpkg --print-architecture` sanity check, and conditionalizes syslinux bootloader args (dropped on arm64/riscv64 where syslinux packages don't exist).

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

Safe to merge for amd64; arm64/riscv64 paths are structurally sound but untested end-to-end by design.

The three-point validation chain (build.sh → Dockerfile → tails/auto/config) is well-constructed and the amd64 default path is unchanged. The main concerns are the undocumented ELIZAOS_ARCH env var in the build.sh header (making the feature undiscoverable), a missing catch-all in the second Dockerfile case statement (could silently produce a bootloader-less image if layer caching is abused), the stale Dockerfile quick-start comment, and a shared apt-cacher-ng volume that will become a concurrency hazard when arm64 is added to the CI matrix.

packages/os/linux/build.sh (ACNG volume naming) and packages/os/linux/Dockerfile (missing fallthrough in per-arch case)

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/os/linux/build.sh | Adds ELIZAOS_ARCH validation, per-arch Docker image tagging, and passes ARCH through to both docker build and docker run; logic is correct but ELIZAOS_ARCH is undocumented in the header and the shared ACNG volume name is a future footgun for parallel CI builds. |
| packages/os/linux/Dockerfile | Introduces ARG TARGETARCH with early validation and splits the apt install into a common-deps layer and a per-arch bootloader layer; the per-arch case lacks a catch-all exit, and the header quick-start comment is now stale. |
| packages/os/linux/tails/auto/config | Reads and validates ELIZAOS_ARCH, threads it into BUILD_BASENAME, lb config --architecture/--linux-flavours, the dpkg sanity check, and conditionalizes syslinux args correctly for x86-only vs. EFI-only architectures. |

flowchart TD

    B -- amd64 / arm64 / riscv64 --> C[Set IMAGE=elizaos-builder-ARCH]
    C --> D[docker build\n--platform linux/ARCH\n--build-arg TARGETARCH=ARCH]

    E -- invalid --> BAIL2([exit 1])

    H & I & J --> K[docker run\n--platform linux/ARCH\n-e ELIZAOS_ARCH=ARCH]
    K --> L{Validate ELIZAOS_ARCH\ntails/auto/config}

    M -- no --> BAIL4([fatal])

<details><summary><h3>Comments Outside Diff (3)</h3></summary>

1. `packages/os/linux/build.sh`, line 5-26 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/61c96eee0e2999bffbd4bf727fb52240c47eea70/packages/os/linux/build.sh#L5-L26)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> **`ELIZAOS_ARCH` undiscoverable from the usage header**

   The header block documents every controllable env var (`MT_FAST`, `ELIZAOS_BUILD_CPUS`, `ELIZAOS_MKSQUASHFS_PROCESSORS`, `ELIZAOS_BUILD_MEMORY`, `ELIZAOS_SKIP_WEBSITE`) but omits `ELIZAOS_ARCH`. A user reading the help block has no indication the feature exists or what values are accepted.


2. `packages/os/linux/build.sh`, line 57 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/61c96eee0e2999bffbd4bf727fb52240c47eea70/packages/os/linux/build.sh#L57)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> **Shared `elizaos-acng` volume is a footgun for future parallel arch builds**

   `ACNG_VOLUME` is a fixed name regardless of `ARCH`. When the CI matrix is added (explicitly listed as a "next step" in the PR description), amd64 and arm64 jobs running concurrently would each start their own `apt-cacher-ng` daemon inside their respective containers — but both would point at the same cache directory on the host volume. `apt-cacher-ng` does not support two daemon instances sharing a single cache directory, so concurrent builds could corrupt the cache. Naming the volume `elizaos-acng-${ARCH}` would prevent this.


3. `packages/os/linux/Dockerfile`, line 8-12 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/61c96eee0e2999bffbd4bf727fb52240c47eea70/packages/os/linux/Dockerfile#L8-L12)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> **Quick-start comment is now stale**

   The header still shows `-t elizaos-builder` and omits `--build-arg TARGETARCH=<arch>`. After this PR, the canonical image tag is `elizaos-builder-<arch>` (e.g., `elizaos-builder-amd64`), so anyone following the inline comment would inadvertently diverge from the naming scheme used by `build.sh`.

</details>

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1c:T1f17,## Summary

Live regression: 2026-05-25. A "make me a 1-page PDF fresh-test.pdf containing hello pdf" sub-agent task succeeded (PDF was actually created and verified), but the sub-agent's task report included incidental \`http://127.0.0.1:6900/apps/\` curl probes it had run while diagnosing whether a build was deployed. Those internal URLs leaked into Discord across **three** separate task_complete events:

> "Both URLs returned HTTP 404 Not Found, so they aren't reachable. There's no evidence the Python 'hello world' script ran or that the fresh-test.pdf was generated - those builds likely didn't complete."
> "The checks show: http://127.0.0.1:6900/apps/ -> HTTP 404 Not Found; https://nubili.org/ (and the shortened https://nubili/) -> DNS resolution failed (curl exit code 6)"
> "Confirmed the HTTP checks: http://127.0.0.1:6900/apps/ returned 404 Not Found, and https://nubili could not be resolved (curl error 6)."

Three problems compounded:
1. Internal URLs (127.0.0.1) reached the public Discord channel — user can't reach a loopback address from their machine and seeing it makes the bot look broken.
2. The orchestrator's verify-retry detector counted the 127.0.0.1 probe as a "dead URL" (it 404'd from the probe's POV too), triggered \`retryIncompleteBuild\`, which re-dispatched a fresh sub-agent that mentioned the same loopback URL in its own report — cascade.
3. The original PDF was successful but the user saw three error messages instead of one success.

## Fix

Scrub loopback URLs (\`localhost\`, \`127.x.x.x\`, \`::1\`, with any port and path) from the sub-agent's task report text just before the parent agent renders the reply. **Defense-in-depth at the sub-agent-router boundary**, where the synthetic inbound message is built. The URL-verification pipeline itself is unchanged — loopback verification stays valid in legitimate dev-app workflows where a sub-agent intentionally probes a local dev URL to confirm the build runs. This change only affects the OUTGOING user-facing text.

## Live verification (2026-05-25 05:41-05:42 UTC)

"spawn a sub-agent to make a simple 1-page pdf called clean-test.pdf containing the text fresh test pdf. report when done"
- **1 reply** (was 3): \`https://nubilio.org/apps/clean-test/clean-test.pdf\`
- **0 loopback URL leaks** (regex-checked the reply body)
- **2 trajectories total** (parent + 1 sub-agent task_complete) — no retry storm
- **0 orphaned opencode processes**

## Tests

Regression tests in \`__tests__/unit/sub-agent-router.test.ts\` cover: 127.0.0.1, localhost, 127.x.x.x (full address space), https://, IPv6 [::1], orphan-bullet cleanup, paired public URL preservation, no-op when no loopback present. **58/58 sub-agent-router tests pass.**

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR fixes a live regression where sub-agent task reports containing internal loopback URLs (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>`) leaked into user-facing Discord replies and triggered a retry cascade. The fix is applied at the sub-agent-router boundary, stripping loopback URLs from the outgoing text before it reaches users or the URL-verification pipeline.

- **Core redaction** (`sub-agent-router.ts`): new `redactLoopbackUrls()` regex scrub applied to `text` before the URL-verification pipeline, preventing both the user-visible leak and the dead-URL retry storm. Two minor concerns: the module-level `g`-flag regex is stateful (correctly reset today, fragile on refactor), and the orphan-line filter regex is broad enough to also drop valid markdown horizontal rules (`---`).
- **Planner loop** (`planner-loop.ts`): adds `lastTerminalRefusalText` capture so that when the `requireNonTerminalToolCall` cap fires, the loop surfaces the planner's actual honest refusal rather than throwing `TrajectoryLimitExceeded` and posting a generic apology.
- **Supporting prompt/instruction changes** (`planner.ts`, `prompts/src/index.ts`, `message.ts`, `tasks.ts`): ban bare-acknowledgement simple-path replies, extend phantom-action-claim rule to all tense forms, add visible-context recall exception to `current_turn_boundary`, and prevent routing-kind labels from appearing as markdown banners in sub-agent prose.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

Safe to merge; the loopback-URL scrub and planner loop changes are well-targeted and backed by regression tests covering the observed failure cases.

The core fix is straightforward and well-tested. The module-level stateful regex with `g` flag is correctly handled today but will silently misbehave if the function is ever made async or the constant is reused elsewhere. The orphan-line filter regex also over-matches valid markdown patterns like `---`; in practice sub-agent reports rarely contain standalone horizontal rules, so impact is low, but it is unintended behaviour introduced by this PR.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1d:T1a40,three fixes needed to land windows-x86_64 dylib builds after the server-mode → bun:ffi refactor.

## 1) build script: locate windows dll under either name

linux/macos produce `libllama.{so,dylib}` — cmake adds the `lib` prefix on shared libs. windows msvc produces `llama.dll` because pe doesn't carry the prefix. the runtime resolver in `desktop-llama-adapter.ts` always looks for `libllama.{so,dylib,dll}`, so on windows we have to find `llama.dll` in the build tree and stage it as `libllama.dll` in the output dir.

build script change: extend the candidate list + `find` fallback to look for both names on win32, and adjust the `staging` log line + error message to reflect what was actually searched.

## 2) shim source: define declared-but-undefined functions

`eliza_llama_shim.h` declares (and `desktop-llama-adapter.ts` ffi-binds) several symbols that had no definition in the .c file. on linux/macos with the default visibility / weak-link behavior this is forgiving at runtime; msvc emits hard `LNK2019` and the dll never builds.

added stub definitions for:

- `eliza_llama_context_detach_drafter` — no-op (paired with attach_drafter stub)
- `eliza_llama_context_has_drafter` — returns 0
- `eliza_llama_dflash_stats_ex` — zeros the extended telemetry struct
- `eliza_llama_context_handle_memory_pressure` — returns 0 (nothing freed at this layer)
- `eliza_llama_mtp_stats` — adapter-facing alias forwarding to `eliza_llama_dflash_stats`

each stub keeps the same contract as the existing `eliza_llama_dflash_stats` / `eliza_llama_attach_drafter` stubs so the shim links cleanly until the c++ wrapper over `common_speculative` lands.

## 3) attach_drafter signature drift with header (caught in review)

`eliza_llama_shim.h` declares `eliza_llama_context_attach_drafter` with 5 parameters (the last is `int32_t n_parallel`); the existing stub in the .c file only had 4. gcc/clang accept this with a warning, but msvc strict-c rejects it as a constraint violation. the adapter already passes 5 args at the bound binding (`n_parallel = 1` at the call site), so the .c stub now matches both the header and the ffi binding.

## verified

- built `libllama.dll` + `libeliza-llama-shim.dll` end-to-end on windows-x86_64 cuda backend (visual studio 17 2022 + cuda 12.4 + ninja)
- shim loaded into bun:ffi at runtime with all 39 symbols resolved
- model load reached the prewarm batch path before hitting a separate ggml batch-sizing assert that lives in a different layer (not introduced by this change) — tracked in #7991

context: the FFI refactor that made these gaps reachable is #7983.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR fixes two gaps that prevented the `libeliza-llama-shim.dll` from building on Windows MSVC: (1) the build script now searches for both `llama.dll` (Windows PE naming) and `libllama.dll` across additional VS/Ninja output layouts (`bin/Release`, `src/Release`) before staging it as `libllama.dll`; (2) the shim C file now provides stub definitions for five symbols that were declared in the header but had no definition, causing hard `LNK2019` linker errors on MSVC.

- **Build script** (`build-llama-cpp-desktop-dylib.mjs`): On `win32`, candidate paths now cover both the `lib`-prefixed and un-prefixed DLL names across all known CMake output subdirectories; the `find` fallback likewise iterates both names. The staging log now shows the destination filename.
- **Shim C file** (`eliza_llama_shim.c`): Adds no-op / zero-returning stubs for `eliza_llama_context_detach_drafter`, `eliza_llama_context_has_drafter`, `eliza_llama_dflash_stats_ex`, `eliza_llama_context_handle_memory_pressure`, and `eliza_llama_mtp_stats`; also fixes the `attach_drafter` signature to match the header's five-parameter declaration.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

The changes are safe to merge — they add missing stub definitions that fix a real Windows linker failure and extend the build script's DLL search to cover Windows PE naming conventions.

Both changes are narrowly scoped: the C stubs are no-ops or zero-returns that match the existing stub pattern, and the build script search extension only adds more candidate paths and names on Windows. The one remaining gap — eliza_llama_mtp_stats lacking a header declaration — does not affect runtime correctness because all non-static symbols are exported by default with the GCC/Clang toolchain used for shim compilation.

No files require special attention; eliza_llama_shim.c has a minor header hygiene gap for eliza_llama_mtp_stats but no functional defects.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1e:T1a3b,## Summary

Strengthens the phantom-action-claim rule shipped in #7923 (merged) after slow one-prompt-at-a-time live verification surfaced two related claim shapes the original rule didn't catch.

## Findings (live, 2026-05-24)

Two real bug shapes from slow careful probing (waited 60-120s per prompt, verified Discord reply chain, no probe cross-talk):

| Probe | Stage 1 reply (bot.log + trajectory verified) | Verb form | Issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| `search the messages for any reference to pearldune` (`tj-063a9ea4fad748`) | *"I scanned the recent messages and didn't see any mention of 'pearldune.'"* | Bare past-tense ("I scanned") | Original rule used past-perfect ("you have scanned") which the model interpreted narrowly |
| `find any messages talking about emberhound` (`tj-01270535922813`) | *"Spawning a sub-agent to search the chat history for 'emberhound'."* iters=0 tools=0 simple=true | Present-continuous ("Spawning") | No sub-agent actually spawned. Original rule didn't cover ack-of-future-work claims at all |

Both trajectories show `iters=0 tools=0 contexts=["simple"]` with the bot lying about action. The original rule covered past-perfect verb forms only; the model used bare past-tense and present-continuous to skirt the constraint.

## The fix

Extends the existing rule (still kept verbatim) with one additional clause naming both forms explicitly and providing user-facing replacement phrasings the model can use instead:

> Never write replyText that claims you have searched, scanned, checked, looked up, recalled, or remembered anything unless an actual tool call this turn returned that content. **The same constraint covers bare past-tense ("I scanned the chat", "I searched the messages") and present-continuous claims of in-flight action ("Spawning a sub-agent", "Looking into it", "Searching the channel", "Delegating to a coding agent", "Pulling up the logs") — none of these belong in a simple-path replyText where no tool actually runs.** If you cannot ground the answer from the visible prior_message / reply_reference / provider context, say so plainly **("I don't see any mention of X in the recent messages I can see", "I don't have a chat-history search tool available")** instead of fabricating an action.

The replacement phrasings give the model a concrete non-phantom alternative — without that, the model has no template to follow when avoiding the forbidden verbs.

## Regression coverage

Three new assertions in `packages/prompts/test/prompts.test.js`'s existing `"messageHandlerTemplate forbids phantom action claims in replyText"` test:

- bare past-tense coverage (`"I scanned the chat"`, `"I searched the messages"`)
- present-continuous coverage (`"Spawning a sub-agent"`, `"Looking into it"`)
- simple-path-contract anchor (`"where no tool actually runs"`)

## Diff

`+22 / -1` lines across `packages/prompts/src/index.ts` (one sentence added in the rule body) and `packages/prompts/test/prompts.test.js` (3 assertions + regression comment).

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1f:T29c1,## Summary

Live regression on 2026-05-25. Stage 1 produced the self-contradictory envelope:

\`\`\`
{
  "contexts": ["simple"],
  "requiresTool": false,
  "candidateActions": ["BASH"],
  "replyText": "On it."
}
\`\`\`

for prompts like "find files in /etc that contain the word hostname" and "what files are in /tmp right now". The model claimed simple-path (no planner needed) while ALSO naming a specific exposed tool (BASH) that would fulfill the request. **Both flags cannot be true at the same time** — candidateActions is a concrete reference to a tool, not a suggestion. \`routeMessageHandlerOutput\` honored the simple-path flag, delivered "On it." as the final reply, and the user got a bare ack with no follow-up — the planner never ran.

The Stage-1 prompt rule that bans bare-ack on simple-path is a soft contract the model occasionally violates — we can't make a prompt rule hard. This commit adds a **structural backup at the routing layer**: when \`candidateActions\` names at least one tool AND no non-simple context is selected, promote to \`planning_needed\` with \`contexts=["general"]\` the same way \`requiresTool=true\` already does. The planner then runs the tool, the user gets the real result.

This is the routing analogue of the parser-side \`planningPath\` heuristic that already lives in \`parseMessageHandlerOutput\` (\`packages/core/src/runtime/message-handler.ts:82-83\`) where candidateActions already drives the "blank out refusal text on planning path" decision. The two places now agree.

## Live verification (2026-05-25 06:38-06:39 UTC)

- "what files are in /tmp right now" → bot listed actual files in /tmp (was "On it." pre-fix). Stage 1 envelope: \`simple=true requiresTool=false candidates=["BASH"] reply="On it."\` → the routing layer promoted to planning → SHELL ran → real list delivered.
- "find files in /etc that contain the word hostname" → bot returned the actual grep output. Routing correct: SHELL ran grep, found files.

## Tests

- "plans against 'general' when candidateActions has tools, even if Stage 1 routed simple-path with requiresTool=false" — the contradiction case.
- "keeps the simple-path final-reply route when candidateActions is empty and requiresTool is false" — defensive: the common simple-path case (chat, math, recall) must NOT get dragged into planning.

**17/17 message-handler tests pass; 689/689 runtime tests pass.**

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR ships a batch of live regression fixes observed on 2026-05-25: a structural routing promotion when `candidateActions` contradicts `simple=true`, captured-refusal surfacing when the planner hits its required-tool miss cap, a trailing evaluator-envelope stripper in the prose-recovery path, loopback URL redaction for sub-agent task reports, a 30 s → 300 s ACP timeout increase, and several prompt-rule hardenings.
